ParlaMint-FR_2018-04-22-O1206.u10	If we support the creation of a single document for children, it is important not to erase the mention "republican identity title", which currently exists for children born in France. This distinction makes it possible to recall that, according to article 21-7 of the Civil Code, children born in France are considered to be, if they so wish – since they will always be able to renounce that nationality at a later date – already a party to the French people and nation – article 3 of the Constitution. The extension of the mention of "republican identity title" to the brothers and sisters of the person concerned also ensures that the documents of the children of the family are not described differently, and also allows, in the name of the preservation of the family unit, to specify and recognize that if a child already has a strong connection with the people and the French nation, by extension and by the family ties that bind them, his brothers and sisters also have de facto one. In the name of the best interests of the child enshrined in article 3 of the International Convention on the Rights of the Child, and in order to prevent children of the same family from having different documents, unite them by giving the child born in France and his brother the same title.
ParlaMint-FR_2018-04-22-O1206.u11	Mrs Bénédicte Taurine has the floor to support Amendment No. 976.
ParlaMint-FR_2018-04-22-O1206.u12	As we have said, we are in favour of Article 24, which aims to simplify the very complex provisions of the Code of Entry and Residence of Aliens and the right of asylum – CESEDA. Indeed, the creation of a single circulation document lasting five years makes it possible to clarify and simplify the procedures for all parents. However, we remind you that there were two different documents before. One of them was entitled "Republican Identity Title". It made it clear that some foreign children were born in France. This distinction makes it possible to recall that, according to article 21-7 of the Civil Code, children born in France are considered to be, if they so wish, already a party to the French people and nation. We believe that it is necessary to preserve the reference to "republican identity title", without altering the procedure and conditions of attribution proposed by the Government. If this common-sense amendment were not adopted, it would have to be seen as a serious regression by the Government, which would like to eliminate the potential membership of the nation of children born in France to foreign parents, which means de facto questioning the law of the land.

ParlaMint-FR_2018-04-22-O1206.u22	By this amendment, proposed by CIMADE, we wish to put an end to the distinction between children of persons with different residence permits. Article 24 provides for a single movement document allowing foreign minors to return to France after a trip abroad, i.e. in practice to make school trips, which is valid for five years for all, with a few exceptions, which we propose to delete. Indeed, for parents who hold certain residence permits, such as employees, students, or sick persons treated in France, the validity of this movement document may be less than five years. We consider this unjustified discrimination because it could involve, each year, costs of renewal of 45 euros and additional steps for all parents, even though the prefecture can still withdraw a circulation document, if it considers that the validity period is too long, in view of the parents' stay. In the interests of the best interests of the child as enshrined in article 3 of the International Convention on the Rights of the Child, these distinctions and burdens must be stopped, which hinder the right of such minors to a normal life.

ParlaMint-FR_2018-04-22-O1206.u32	Paragraph 4 of article 26 should be deleted, since it is intended to give the OFII the possibility to keep doctors recruited under contract who carry out medical examinations until the age of 73, by way of derogation from the law of the civil service. It is a matter of dealing with the lack of doctors working in this institution. After trying twice to introduce this amendment, the Government seems to have found a new legislative vehicle. However, we will vote against this article for two main reasons. First, between the ages of 67 and 73, it is very likely that physicians will no longer practice outside OFII, thereby keeping them away from advances in knowledge and medical practice. This provision seems to us all the less relevant as the Government refers to a single precedent: the raising of the legal retirement age to 73 years had been provided for in a 2016 law, which had not been the subject of an impact assessment. Secondly, we believe that recruitment is more appropriate, as new doctors would be better able to guarantee quality medical examinations. The proposed solution therefore does not really address the underlying problem of the lack of human and financial resources allocated to OFII. These are the reasons why we are proposing this deletion amendment.

To an already complicated access to the labour market is added the need to provide a contract or a promise of employment. In fact, it is clear from the Robert Schuman Foundation's study on migration policies that the Regional Directorate for Enterprise, Competition, Consumer, Labour and Employment – DIRECCTE – granted only one authorisation to work for an asylum seeker in 2015. ParlaMint-FR_2018-04-22-O1206.u126	This amendment, which has been reworked along the lines mentioned in the committee, aims to allow asylum seekers who have not received a response from OFPRA to enter the labour market within six months of the submission of their application, rather than nine months as provided for in the legislation currently in force. Indeed, the nine-month period seems disproportionate, since keeping asylum seekers inactive is detrimental to all, impairs their integration capacities, does not allow them to support themselves and promotes undeclared employment. This measure takes up proposal No 47 of our colleague Aurélien Taché's report on the successful integration of foreigners in France. Asylum seekers would naturally remain subject to the rules of ordinary law applicable to foreign workers for the issuance of a work permit. However, the amendment provides that the administrative authority shall have a period of two months from the date of receipt of the application for a work permit to ensure that the employment of a foreigner complies with the conditions of common law for access to the labour market. In the absence of notification within that period, the authorisation shall then be deemed to have been acquired and shall apply for the duration of the asylum-seeker's right to remain.
ParlaMint-FR_2018-04-22-O1206.u127	I call Mr Aurélien Taché to support Amendment No. 1053.
ParlaMint-FR_2018-04-22-O1206.u128	This amendment is the result of the 72 proposals in the report that I submitted to the Government with a view to strengthening integration. It aims to facilitate the access of asylum seekers to the labour market. What is the situation today, ladies and gentlemen? Asylum seekers can apply for permission to work nine months after applying for asylum, and the Department of Labour sometimes takes as long to answer. In fact, it is therefore extremely difficult for the applicants to work during the asylum procedure, which considerably affects the integration of all those who will remain in France, which must be our priority. This legislation, one of the most restrictive in Europe, explains that we have some of Europe's worst results in terms of access to employment for foreigners who are regularly admitted to our country: only one in three find jobs in the five years following their arrival in France. [[Protests on the benches of the LR group]] Yet those who are forced into exile do not come to ask for assistance or charity, nor the RSA as Mr.Wauquiez seems to think,...

ParlaMint-FR_2018-04-22-O1206.u130	[[Exclaims on the benches of the LR group]] ... they want to regain dignity, which starts with being able to work. Everyone I have met in the camps or in the reception centres has a very bad bearing on the forced idleness in which our asylum procedure takes them. It is therefore time to give them much wider access to work.
ParlaMint-FR_2018-04-22-O1206.u131	The French don't think like you!
ParlaMint-FR_2018-04-22-O1206.u132	If the amendment is adopted, those persons may apply for permission to work no later than six months after submitting their application, which corresponds to the objective set by the Minister for the total duration of the procedure. In the absence of a response from the Department of Labour after two months, this authorization will be deemed to be acquired. It would be a major step forward to enshrine the right to work of asylum seekers who would not have received a response to their request within a reasonable time, and a major step towards the integration of refugees who want and need to be able to work as quickly as possible. Ladies and gentlemen, let us not add the pain of downgrading to that of exile.

Amendment No 169, on the other hand, seeks to amend paragraph 6, in order for the Defender of Rights to give his opinion on certain points in the redrafting of the legislative part of the CESEDA resulting from the ordinances.

ParlaMint-FR_2018-04-22-O1206.u250	Mrs Mathilde Panot has the floor to support Amendment No. 982.
ParlaMint-FR_2018-04-22-O1206.u251	With this amendment, we want to guarantee the conditions for issuing the so-called "visitor" residence permit, which the Government wishes to tighten. This article is intended to simplify, but it in fact confirms a tightening of the conditions for granting this residence permit by the administration. The "visitor" residence card allows people to come and visit their families and relatives. This seems quite normal: the exercise of this right should not be hindered. So far, the issue of this card was subject to a resource requirement and a commitment not to work in France. But under the guise of simplification, the Government adds two conditions: a health insurance covering the duration of his stay, and the explicit exclusion of benefits and allowances, which cannot be taken into account for the calculation of resources. We therefore believe that this is not a simplification measure, but an unjustified restriction on the right of residence. That is why we propose, by this amendment, to delete this article. The French language should not be emptied of its meaning: "hardening" is not "simplification". We see neither the necessity nor the usefulness of this article, for people with family ties in France must be able to enter and leave the territory without undue hindrance.

ParlaMint-FR_2018-04-22-O1206.u253	For this government, the urgency is not to improve the conditions of the right to stay for those who hold a work permit – students and cardholders with the term "private and family life". It is not urgent to tackle the many difficulties these people face in issuing or renewing their residence permits. No, for this government, there is an urgent need to improve the conditions for issuing certificates for a very small number of people, those who are subject to intra-group mobility within Europe. This attitude of improving conditions for persons who represent less than 1 per cent of the first titles issued, without addressing the conditions applicable to the remaining 262000, is somewhat problematic. ParlaMint-FR_2018-04-22-O1206.u255	Section 30 includes perhaps the most cynical provisions of this bill, of which the child will be a collateral victim. Indeed, by this article, you punish the child if his or her French parent does not support his or her needs. This happens, unfortunately: in these cases, the other parent, often a mother who is a victim of violence, is placed in a precarious situation, even when she takes care of the children. In this case, you punish the child by depriving him of his identity and the rights attached thereto, as long as the administration has not recognized the truth of filiation, which is questioned a priori when one of the parents is a foreigner. With this provision, you will temporarily create children without identity, ghost children. CIMADE reported the example of one person who obtained eight receipts in two years during the paternity investigation. During these two years, doubt persisted on the surname and nationality of the child, which was thus without identity during this period. We oppose this article, which shows the will to punish even children: that is why we call for its abolition.
ParlaMint-FR_2018-04-22-O1206.u256	I call Mr Lecoq to support Amendment No. 835.
ParlaMint-FR_2018-04-22-O1206.u257	Article 30 requires the French parent who has recognized the child, and not only the foreign parent, to demonstrate that he or she is making an effective contribution to the child's maintenance and education. On the one hand, it appears that the evidence of effective participation in the maintenance and upbringing of the child is difficult to report and subject to a wide margin of interpretation on the part of both the prefectural authorities and the administrative judge. On the other hand, this new condition would doubly penalize children whose French parent would be absent, which would prevent the foreign parent taking care of them from being regularized. The scheme does not take into account the possibility of an absence of ties independent of the will of the foreign parent, resulting from the behaviour of the French parent – violence, abandonment, etc. Finally, such monitoring could lead public authorities to target in particular certain persons, where appropriate by seeking administrative or judicial investigations on the basis of prohibited discrimination criteria such as origin or nationality. In this regard, we share the concern of the Defender of Rights about the significant risk of stigmatization or even discriminatory policy towards a class of persons on the basis of their nationality and origin. As part of the investigation of the complaints before him, the Defender of Rights was informed of internal notes to the prefectural authorities concerning the fight against this type of fraud and, as a criterion for the identification of suspect persons, of the nationality of the foreign nationals concerned and even of the country of origin of the French father.
ParlaMint-FR_2018-04-22-O1206.u258	Mrs Bénédicte Taurine has the floor to support Amendment No. 985.
ParlaMint-FR_2018-04-22-O1206.u259	The aim of this abolition amendment is to preserve the rights of French children, who cannot be relegated to the status of second-class citizens. Article 30 further restricts the conditions for issuing a residence permit to foreign parents of a French child. As it stands, Article L. 313-11 of the CESEDA provides that foreign parents of a French child may, under the condition that they contribute effectively to the maintenance and education of the child – or of the children – obtain a residence permit as a parent of a French child. With this bill, in order to be able to issue a residence permit to the foreign parent of the French child, it will also be necessary for the other parent, of French nationality, to contribute effectively to the maintenance and education of the child. This absurd rule will make the situation of the foreign parent more precarious, and will isolate minor children, since you refuse to allow the parent who actually cares for the child to accompany the minor. Under cover of the fight against fraudulent recognition of the link of filiation of French nationals, you choose to endanger French minors. This provision is therefore contrary to the idea of our parliamentary group of the best interests of the children.

ParlaMint-FR_2018-04-22-O1206.u304	I have before me six amendments, No 405 corrected, 404, 400, 401, 407 and 406, which can be presented in a consolidated format. Mrs Valérie Boyer has the floor to support them.
ParlaMint-FR_2018-04-22-O1206.u305	This series of amendments deals specifically with fraudulent marriages. Amendment No. 405, corrected, which aims to train civil-state officers, is the result of the bill of 21 February 2018 to strengthen the fight against fraudulent marriages. According to article L. 2122-32 of the General Code of Local and Regional Authorities, "The mayor and the deputies are civil registrars. However, article R. 2122-10 of the same Code provides that the mayor may delegate to one or more officials holding the commune all or part of his duties as a registrar. According to the Code, members of municipal councils have the right to training appropriate to their duties. More than ever before, we must strengthen this right to training by providing civil-state officers with training on the detection of fraudulent marriages. Although this measure will be the responsibility of the municipalities, it fulfils the conditions laid down by the Constitutional Council with regard to respect for the constitutional principle of the free administration of local and regional authorities: it is in fact only part of the more general and pre-existing obligation of training adapted to the functions of elected officials. Amendment No 404 allows me to welcome the work of Marc Le Fur and Philippe Goujon in the field of combating fraudulent marriages, and to recall that in 2017, 17500 people acquired French nationality through marriage. It seems obvious that controlling immigration requires combating marriages of convenience, which must be the responsibility of mayors and prosecutors. Although the fundamental right to marriage is recognized in Article 12 of the European Convention for the Protection of Fundamental Rights and Freedoms, doing nothing would be tantamount to being complicit in the channels and thereby strengthening trafficking in human beings. French law currently allows a person of foreign nationality who is in an irregular situation on French soil to marry. Indeed, if there are several conditions to be met in order to be able to marry – age, consent, monogamy – none concern the regularity of the stay on French soil. The current legislation therefore leaves a gap in the system for combating white or grey marriages open. It is important to remedy this: this is the meaning of Amendment No 404. The aim of Amendment No. 400 is to make it compulsory to refer the matter to the public prosecutor in case of doubt as to the sincerity of the marriage. I would remind you that there are 300,000 illegal aliens in our country. Today, the mayors must conduct the hearing of the prospective spouses, prior to the publication of the banns, in order in particular to detect the lack of real and free matrimonial intention of the candidates for marriage. Unfortunately, for fear, ideological reasons or even mere ignorance of the law, many town halls do not conduct such hearings. The financial stakes of these fraudulent marriages are such that some individuals do not hesitate to threaten the civil-state officer who would have the misfortune to oppose the union they plan. He is often insulted. According to some media, prices would vary according to the country of origin: 8000 euros when it is sub-Saharan Africa, around 15,000 euros for the Maghreb and 30,000 euros for China... The money is, after the marriage, distributed between the branch and the French spouse. Today, some civil-state officers, too often exposed and insufficiently supported by the prefecture, do not dare to denounce this type of marriage. Even if they did, the police prefecture wouldn't do it. In fact, I asked the prefecture of Marseilles about this and did not get any answers. However, many mayors have denounced these practices, either because they have information, or because the marital intention seems to them to be absent from the marital file. This is why it is important to act more effectively against these fraudulent marriages: on the one hand, the law puts in place strict conditions for entering into a marriage, on the other hand, it allows illegal immigrants to enter into the same marriage. This contradiction, this difference in treatment may seem unbearable to French citizens. For this reason, I wish to amend the beginning of article 175 (2) of the Civil Code: "Where there are serious indications suggesting, if any, in the light of the hearing provided for in article 63, that the marriage envisaged is liable to be annulled under article 146 or article 180, the registrar may refer the matter to the public prosecutor without delay. I would like this option to become an obligation and for the civil-state officer to systematically seize the public prosecutor. Amendment No 401 wants to oblige the public prosecutor seized by the mayor to suspend automatically the celebration of a union in case of suspicion of marriage of convenience, and to extend the period of stay. Also in order to prevent a marriage for the purpose of obtaining or obtaining a residence permit or French nationality, it is necessary to oblige the Public Prosecutor's Office, seized by the mayor, to suspend automatically the celebration of a union in case of suspicion of marriage of convenience. Currently, the suspension period is one month renewable. I would like to increase it to three renewable months, leaving more time to be provided by the Republic to carry out an investigation in order to establish the attempt to commit one of the offences described by the CESEDA, in order to initiate possible prosecution. Amendment No 407 is intended to appoint a referent elected representative as "fraudful marriages". Indeed, the mayor and his deputies are civil state officers.
ParlaMint-FR_2018-04-22-O1206.u306	That's what she said before.
ParlaMint-FR_2018-04-22-O1206.u307	They carry out this task on behalf of the State, under the authority of the public prosecutor. Also with a view to strengthening the fight against fraudulent marriages, in each municipality, district or sector, the mayor must be able to appoint one or more elected civil-state officers, so that they are referents in matters of fraudulent marriages. They would then be responsible for advising other registrars in the conduct of mandatory hearings and in the detection of marriages envisaged for a purpose other than marriage union. This would facilitate and improve the expertise required in case of doubt, particularly in the conduct of future bride and groom hearings, without increasing the costs of the community. To conclude on Amendment No 406, the General Code of Local and Regional Authorities provides, as I have indicated, that the mayor and his deputies are the only civil state officers. However, the same code leaves, as I have also indicated, the possibility for the mayor to delegate to one or more officials holding the commune certain functions which he performs as a registrar. According to the same code, members of the municipal council are entitled to training adapted to their duties. It seems to me more important than ever to strengthen this right to training by providing civil registry officers with training on the detection of fraudulent marriages. Even if this measure were to be the responsibility of the municipalities, it would meet the conditions laid down by the Constitutional Council with regard to respect for the constitutional principle of the free administration of local and regional authorities, since it is only part of the more general and pre-existing obligation of training adapted to the functions of the elected representatives. Ladies and gentlemen, this series of amendments seems to me to be very important in the fight against fraudulent marriage patterns, which must cease to operate in France and prosper there. The communal elected representatives are no longer under pressure, no longer feeling threatened or no longer being insulted as is often the case when they conduct these hearings. They must no longer be alone in the face of prefectures, which unfortunately do not inform them of the follow-up given to their reports.

ParlaMint-FR_2018-04-22-O1206.u356	I call Mr Bastien Lachaud to support Amendment No. 987.
ParlaMint-FR_2018-04-22-O1206.u357	This is an appeal amendment, by which we wish to guarantee the full independence of the medical profession, which will have to assess the state of health of a person applying for a "ill foreigner" residence permit. Prior to 2016, the Regional Health Agency's physicians had the function of reviewing and advising on the health status of the person applying for the title, in particular with regard to the provision of care and the characteristics of the health care system in the country of origin, in order to determine whether he or she could not receive appropriate treatment there. The lack of a unified practice led to differences of opinion regarding availability and access to treatment in a large number of countries. By law of 7 March 2016, this jurisdiction was therefore transferred from regional health agencies to OFII. The latter has an organic link with the executive, since it is under the supervision of the ministers responsible for immigration and integration. In order to remove the production of this medical opinion from any link with the executive branch and to allow the opinions to be rendered impartially, we propose that this task be entrusted to the Defender of Rights. This is the subject of this experimental amendment.

ParlaMint-FR_2018-04-22-O1206.u418	Being a victim of violence, whether or not they are conjugal, is a tragedy: no one in this House will say otherwise. Of course, we must do everything we can to combat this scourge, which strikes both in France and in the rest of the world. In 2016, in our country, a woman died every three days as a result of the violence suffered by her spouse or former spouse. More specifically, 123 women were killed in 2016, 34 men as well as 25 minor children, victims of one of their parents. More generally, in the world, 70 per cent of women are victims of violence: it is considerable. Most of the time, this violence comes, as in France, from the spouse. It is therefore urgent to give ourselves the means to stop those who abuse their spouse and to punish them as severely as possible, that is for sure. But let us not introduce into the law, in the name of a good intention, too vague terms, which would help to prevent effective control of our migratory flow. In order to extend the protection of victims of domestic and family violence, all limits are abolished. How far will we go? How can the concept of persons belonging to the same family be circumscribed and defined? To what extent is this understood? If a young girl applies for asylum in France after having suffered violence from an uncle or cousin, will she be granted refugee status as of right? You know as I do, the notion of "family" has very different realities in different countries. The term "family", to which this article refers, therefore seems imprecise. That is why it would be useful to delete the article.

ParlaMint-FR_2018-04-22-O1206.u450	I have before me four amendments, Nos 194, 234, 637 and 402, which can be discussed jointly. I call Mr Guillaume Larrivé to support Amendment No. 194.
ParlaMint-FR_2018-04-22-O1206.u451	Although he is late in our debate, this amendment is at the heart of the counter-project proposed by the Republicans. We believe that immigration should be reduced globally, that France should be removed from mass immigration and that immigration flows should be regulated by defining quotas in this Chamber every year, i.e. by setting a ceiling by category and probably also by country. This is an appeal amendment because I know that the macro-majority does not want to reduce immigration. The debates have demonstrated this for six days: it does not want real regulation. However, the Republicans, the first opposition and proposal party, want to submit this amendment aimed at reducing immigration overall and regulating it intelligently.

ParlaMint-FR_2018-04-22-O1206.u455	Mr Eric Ciotti has the floor to support Amendment No. 637.
ParlaMint-FR_2018-04-22-O1206.u456	Mr. Larrivée said: the amendments in this series are at the heart of the project that we are proposing to the French so that the volume of immigration is defined annually by Parliament. With immigration thus controlled and chosen, our country would return to the course of its destiny, choose who it wants to receive and under what conditions. That is the point of my amendment, so that every year Parliament will debate migration issues, take stock of the entries of the last three years and, above all, vote on the number, by category of residence permits, of those it wants to receive. The aim is to establish an annual migration ceiling. [[Applause on the benches of the LR group]] We make it clear that our country has had too many years of immigration. It no longer has the capacity to integrate well – what is indispensable and what we all want – foreigners in our country. We must therefore reduce immigration and give Parliament the means to debate it every year.
ParlaMint-FR_2018-04-22-O1206.u457	Mrs Valérie Boyer has the floor to support Amendment No. 402.
ParlaMint-FR_2018-04-22-O1206.u458	France is generous, but it is not a mosaic or a borderless territory. It is a nation that has the right to choose who can join it, as well as the right to require foreigners to comply with its rules and customs. Regular immigration has reached a record level for forty-three years. In 2017, 262000 residence permits were issued. The economic and social situation in our country must lead us to regulate entry by imposing migration quotas. Last Tuesday, we welcomed the Prime Minister of Canada. Like this country, France must adopt an immigration policy worthy of this name, adapted to its economic and social situation, and thus to its capacity for reception, integration and assimilation. This policy should enable Parliament to define, on an annual basis, in the light of relevant statistics, quotas that will be imposed on the Minister of the Interior and the Prefects. This policy must not be decided in catini, without the knowledge of the French. I stand before you for a democratic system. It is setting ceilings that we can strictly restrict immigration, particularly family reunification. Immigration quotas will take account not only of France's needs, but also of the assimilation capacities of French society. If, in order to ensure this policy, we must move the lines that the interpretation of the European Court of Human Rights gives to the European Convention on Human Rights, we must take our responsibilities before the French people. But our immigration policy must no longer be decided without the knowledge of the French and create all the problems our society is experiencing. If you want successful assimilation and integration, you need a courageous, clear and democratic quota policy.

ParlaMint-FR_2018-04-22-O1206.u564	Since 2006, France has entered into agreements with some countries of emigration or "source States" to better manage migration flows in a concerted manner through official development assistance. Three major principles govern the signing of these agreements: the organisation of legal migration, the fight against illegal immigration and solidarity development. More than ten years later, it is necessary to take stock of this conventional policy and to evaluate its arrangements on an annual basis. That is the whole point of this amendment: I propose that the Government include, in the report referred to in Article 33a, an assessment of bilateral conventions for the management of migration flows and arrangements for voluntary return and reintegration, statistics on unaccompanied minors and the overall guidelines of the national scheme for the reception of asylum seekers.

ParlaMint-FR_2018-04-22-O1206.u572	The material, educational and psychological support provided to minors and young adults facing family, social or educational difficulties which could seriously compromise their balance must be extended to isolated foreign high school students. In order to encourage the integration of these young people into French society, we propose to give the State the opportunity to experiment with the support of these people in their access to the labour market in the first years of their adult life. In the voluntary departments, the representative or the representative of the State could thus experiment with the establishment of care for isolated foreign high school students, up to the age of 21, through the issuance of young major contracts. This is an accompanying scheme designed to enable persons under the age of 21 to find training or work, while at the same time giving them the opportunity to live and support themselves, for those whose families are unable to do so. As isolated young foreigners do not, by definition, have a family that can help them, it is logical in our view that the State, that is, the national community, should take care of them. After providing these people with access to education and the material conditions necessary for this education, the legislator may consider it logical, if successful results are obtained from these experiments, to provide them with access to the labour market.

ParlaMint-FR_2018-04-22-O1206.u651	This amendment is an opportunity to recall the link between marriage and the acquisition of French nationality. "A foreigner or stateless person who enters into a marriage with a spouse of French nationality may, after a period of four years from the date of the marriage, acquire French nationality by declaration provided that on the date of that declaration the community of life, both emotional and material, has not ceased between the spouses since the marriage and that the French spouse has retained his nationality". The period is extended to five years "when the foreigner, at the time of the declaration, either does not justify having resided continuously and regularly for at least three years in France from the date of the marriage, or is not in a position to prove that his French spouse was registered during the duration of their community of living abroad in the register of French nationals established outside France. » We all know the difficulties associated with white marriages. We must fight against marriages of complacency but also protect the victims – I am thinking of women whose husband seeks through marriage to obtain certain advantages: a residence permit in our country, a dwelling or French nationality. To protect these people and avoid marriages of convenience, we propose to extend the time limit for acquiring French nationality through marriage from four to eight years. The longer the delay will be, the less people will be tempted to enter into a white marriage. I want to testify to my experience as mayor. I conducted pre-hearings of the future spouses. When I had doubts, I had repeatedly asked the public prosecutor to prohibit the celebration of marriage. He didn't do anything about it. In the face of this problem, I invite you to take into account the situation of the victims.

ParlaMint-FR_2018-04-22-O1206.u804	Since 1999, article 21-14-1 of the Civil Code provides that the Minister of Defence may grant French nationality by decree to any foreigner engaged in the French armies who has been injured on mission, during or on the occasion of an operational commitment, and who so requests. The purpose of this amendment is to enable the Minister of Culture, under a similar mechanism, to confer French nationality by decree on foreigners who contribute to the cultural influence of our country and to the Francophonie. France would benefit from more and lasting openness to foreign and Francophone talent. Moreover, we have a well-established tradition of welcoming foreign artists. ParlaMint-FR_2018-04-22-O1206.u831	The aim of this amendment is to create a new case of reintegration into French nationality by declaration for persons born in metropolitan France before 1 January 1963 of Algerian parents. At present, these persons are in a different situation from those born after that date, in terms of the rules governing the acquisition of nationality. According to the Ordinance of 21 July 1962 which lays down the consequences of Algeria's independence, persons of ordinary civil status domiciled in Algeria at the time of independence have retained French nationality by right. On the other hand, persons of civil status under local law originating in Algeria were able to retain French nationality only on the double condition that they had signed a declaration of recognition of French nationality and had first established their domicile in France. The Act of 20 December 1966 subsequently amended the Ordinance of 21 July 1962, terminating the possibility of signing a declaration of recognition of French nationality on 23 March 1967 and providing that persons of civil status under local law originating in Algeria who did not subscribe on that date the declaration shall be deemed to have lost French nationality on 1 January 1963. These provisions have affected not only those who have not signed a declaration of recognition, but also their minor children by depriving them of the possibility to benefit from the rules for acquiring ordinary nationality. [[Applause on the benches of the NG group]] Consequently, the situation of children born in France before 1 January 1963 of parents born in Algeria differs from that of children born in France after that date of parents born in Algeria before independence. The consequences of these provisions are sometimes unfair and may even be absurd, especially when one of the children of the same siblings cannot be recognized as French nationality, whereas he was born on French soil and came from the same parents as his brothers and sisters, who are French. This inequality affects only a few hundred individuals, but it is highly symbolic.

ParlaMint-FR_2018-04-22-O1206.u902	Non-EU nationals residing in France, whether in a regular or irregular situation, necessarily pay numerous taxes and duties, indirect – VAT and domestic consumption tax on energy products, which alone account for 55% of the total revenue of the State in 2018 – or direct, such as income tax, which accounts for €73 billion in 2018, or 24% of the revenue of the State. Thus, like all persons present in the territory, they contribute, on an equal basis, to 74 per cent of the total revenue of the State. However, their right to stay is subject to taxes and stamp duties on residence permits with relatively high and problematic rates. Everyone can see the excessive amount that can result from this by consulting the census carried out by the Immigrant Information and Support Group, GISTI. For example, the issuance of a first card for an employee residence permit will cost 269 euros, and its annual renewal will cost as much. The renewal of a residence permit for a sick foreigner will also cost the same amount, etc. We believe that, in view of the tax contribution that already exists for these people, it is necessary to abolish all these taxes and stamp duties on residence permits. As Amendment No 993 says the same thing about the abolition of residence taxes, I believe that it will have been defended.

Some types of files are automatically referred to the residence permit committee, for example refusal to renew a residence permit "privacy and family life" – one of the most protective one-year residence permits – and refusal to issue a residence permit for an alien or a foreigner who has been habitually resident in France for more than ten years. To this end, we propose first of all to extend their referral to all refusals. ParlaMint-FR_2018-04-22-O1206.u960	The purpose of this amendment is to guarantee the independence of women of foreign nationality who live legally in our territory. Many of them depend on their spouses to obtain and retain their residence permits. Their right to remain in our country, therefore, often depends on their marital and family status. As a result, our right does not guarantee their autonomy, and they may find themselves in a vulnerable situation if they are victims of domestic or family violence, since they are totally dependent on their spouse. My colleagues and I have been able to measure their suffering thanks to the associations that have told us their trajectory for many years. Some of these women thus discover the existence of another family upon arrival or suffer violence, but give up leaving their husbands for fear of being deprived of their residence permit. It is also necessary to combat the pressure of spouses, who may blackmail them when they can apply for divorce or repudiation in the country of origin without informing their wives. We therefore propose to put an end to the possibility of withdrawing a residence permit in the event of a breakdown of the common life, because it is too broad a criterion which does not distinguish the particular situations: only the non-renewal could be decided, and provided that the divorce was not pronounced to the wrongs of the spouse of the holder of the residence permit or that there was no repudiation. In addition, we propose to extend the cases of violence taken into account in domestic violence: residence permits would then be automatically renewed in order to guarantee all the necessary protection for women of foreign nationality living in our country. ParlaMint-FR_2018-04-22-O1206.u970	In order to avoid any divergence of interpretation from one prefecture to another, the granting of a temporary residence permit must not be subject to the discretion and absolute power of the administrative authority. The jurisdiction of the administration in this matter must be linked to the law in order to ensure equal treatment of applicants for residence permits. Moreover, the absence of a document to be supplied or the doubt as to the origin of one of them should give rise to a liberal interpretation on the part of the administration, i.e. favouring the granting of the residence permit. However, State practice in terms of granting residence permits, which derives from the so-called Valls circular, requires the applicant to prove ten years of habitual residence in France. This is an excessive condition and we propose to reduce this period to five years in order to encourage the collection of documents to be supplied by the applicant. On the other hand, the reference to "not living in a state of polygamy" should be deleted as it implies that a foreigner would automatically be potentially polygamous. ParlaMint-FR_2018-04-22-O1206.u984	According to INSEE, women are paid 18 per cent less than men on equivalent working time and persons with disabilities are unemployed twice as high as the national average. The Defender of Rights has also rightly reminded us that the age of older persons is the first criterion of discrimination experienced by employees in the private sector. All these situations of inequality and discrimination, which Parliament has repeatedly recognised in order to fight for the promotion of real equality, are, however, not taken into account in the field of exceptional admission to residence. Article L. 313-14 of the CESEDA, and its implementing circular of 28 November 2012, known as the Valls circular, provide, for example, that an employed residence permit may be granted but on condition of having a stable job and for an income equivalent to the SMIC, which creates a threshold effect that does not take into account all the difficulties I have just recalled. [[Applause on the benches of the FI and GDR groups]] We therefore propose to include in the law that prefects will have to consider possible discrimination before making their decision.

ParlaMint-FR_2018-04-22-O1206.u1191	The sociolinguistic workshops – ASL – are based on an approach aimed at the gain in social autonomy of foreign people hosted in neighbourhood associations. Their methodology is based on regular outings in social spaces, movements and the arrival of outside stakeholders, in order to develop the oral and written communicative skills necessary for the autonomous use of these spaces. It also relies on the observation of social spaces divided into four fields: public life, cultural life, civic and social life, and personal life. Therefore, learning is no longer focused solely on language. The mastery of French becomes, in practice, the means of integrating into society and of being autonomous in everyday life. With this amendment, we propose to offer the possibility to the State representative in the department to contribute financially to the development of this so-called "ASL" approach. Several deputies were alerted by the associations. Mr Taché met with some of their representatives, which he mentioned in his report. They stressed the precarious nature of the financing which, decided at the departmental level, is not guaranteed. In addition, these trainings are given only to first-time arrivals. Experience has shown, however, that they should be generalised. This would encourage the integration, both in neighbourhoods and in society as a whole, of certain people who have been in France for several years, but have not had access to these apprenticeships. [[Applause on the benches of the FI group]] This is the aim of the amendment, which aims to promote language proficiency and the integration of the community.

ParlaMint-FR_2018-04-22-O1206.u1202	The amendment aims to introduce annual quotas, voted by Parliament, on the number of immigration visas or residence permits issued by France. As you know, ladies and gentlemen, our country issued some 262,000 residence permits to foreigners last year, which represents an increase of 13.7% in one year. I recall that 227,500 residence permits were granted in 2016, which was already a 4.6% increase over 2015. That is why it seems to me more than necessary for Parliament, composed of representatives of the nation, to fix each year the maximum number of people who will be allowed to enter France the following year. Yes, whether France chooses who has the right or not to settle in its territory seems to me, as many of our compatriots do, a minimum. This is also the prerequisite for integration: we can only integrate new migrants if we have the necessary housing, schools, hospitals and, of course, jobs. This figure will be broken down by category, with the Government and Parliament having to determine the share of economic immigration in relation to family immigration. It does not seem to me to be an incongruous proposal to allow Parliament to deliberate annually on a legal immigration quota, which takes account of employment, housing and the social context. [[Applause on several benches in the LR group]] It is not unusual for French society to bring in those it wishes. ParlaMint-FR_2018-04-22-O1206.u1233	Last year, the daily Libération was entitled "Migration Balance: The Incredible Statistical Improglio". "Unbelievable confusion" and even "wrong" are the words of everyday life, which are certainly not punished, but which speak well of the figures relating to the net migration. Around this question, each one goes by its number and accuses the other in bad faith, in part because INSEE mathematically calculates the migration balance – the difference between the number of entries and exits – which does not reflect reality. In 2017, this balance was positive at 69000 people. However, in order for these figures to be analysed correctly, INSEE would have to provide two different figures: one would record the migration balance of immigration, i.e. the number of immigrants arrived and the number of those who left; the other would account for the migration balance of expatriates, i.e. the difference between the expatriates who left and those who returned. The combination of these balances would make it possible to assess more clearly the composition of the French company. Given the challenges of the migration issue, it would seem relevant to me to have accurate data in hand in order to better control our migratory flow. As for the institute which is responsible for disseminating studies and statistics on our society, it seems to me that it is its duty to be extremely precise. In any case, these figures are necessary for our analyses to be based on reality and not on fictions, feelings – or fantasies, as we like to say on the benches of the far left.

ParlaMint-FR_2018-04-22-O1206.u1318	Mr Loïc Prud'homme has the floor to support Amendment No. 1006.
ParlaMint-FR_2018-04-22-O1206.u1319	By this withdrawal amendment, we propose to guarantee the right to appeal and the procedural rights of persons who have applied for asylum and who have been the subject of a decision to refuse entry, under asylum, into the territory, and of those who have been the subject of a transfer decision, more known as "dublinés". In the present state of the law, a court bis exists for foreigners who are in these two situations. With our proposal, the procedure would be modified on two points. The first would move the trial formation from one judge to three, which is the classical college training. Indeed, the current procedure can unfortunately leave an important part to subjectivity or partiality, and this must be rectified. The training of three judges would allow, through interaction and collective intelligence, a less marked solution of the seal of the individual judging alone to be drawn up. The second point is to impose the presence of the public rapporteur, who guarantees with certainty that the judge acting alone did not mistreat the case or leave no blind spots. In concrete terms, the public rapporteur further guarantees the seriousness of the procedure. Through this common-sense amendment, we wish to realign the right to appeal of asylum-seekers with the common law and thus fully guarantee the right to appeal and the procedural rights of asylum-seekers.

ParlaMint-FR_2018-04-22-O1206.u1335	I call Mr Eric Coquerel to support Amendment No. 1005.
ParlaMint-FR_2018-04-22-O1206.u1336	With this amendment, we propose that the interest of the applicant and the quality of the decision-making by the judge should prevail on the simple organisational facilitation of the functioning of the justice system, which could be achieved by the granting of more human and financial resources to the judicial authority. Indeed, article 34 is a good misconception. It provides for the extension of the time limit for the administrative judge to decide on the legality of a transfer decision under the Dublin Regulation for an alien detained or under house arrest in the course of proceedings from 72 to 96 hours. The problem is that the reading of the impact assessment, the statement of reasons for the article and the provisions themselves demonstrates that this reform is absolutely not envisaged in the interests of the applicant or the quality of the decision-making by the judge. It is only for the executive to avoid red tape. In view of the fact that this article potentially leads to a longer deprivation of the full enjoyment of their rights and freedoms for the foreigners concerned, the amendment seeks to prevent our right from disregarding the European Convention for the Protection of Human Rights and Fundamental Freedoms, in particular Article 5 thereof. France was already condemned in 2016 on this specific point. According to the resulting case law of the European Court of Human Rights, all aspects of detention must be examined within a very short period of time. We thank you, the rapporteur, the Minister of State, for responding to the questions raised by this amendment. ParlaMint-FR_2018-04-22-O1206.u1341	Ladies and gentlemen, this is an amendment of consistency. Article 34, paragraph 10, provides that, in the event of house arrest in the course of proceedings, the administrative judge shall have 72 hours to decide on the transfer decision. However, this time limit should be aligned with the 144-hour time limit applicable to OQTF litigation. In order to correct this error, the Government proposes to simplify the text by making reference to the provisions applicable to OQTFs in this case. He therefore invites you to adopt the amendment.
ParlaMint-FR_2018-04-22-O1206.u1342	I have before me a number of amendments containing additional articles after Rule 34. The Minister has the floor to support Amendment No. 1159, the second amendment.
ParlaMint-FR_2018-04-22-O1206.u1343	Its purpose is to extend the validity of the four-year multi-annual residence card for a period of three months when it is renewed, as is already the case with the resident card. It also provides for testing of the scheme in certain departments for temporary residence cards and multi-year residence cards for a period of less than four years. It would run until 31 December 2020. Such a measure will ensure that foreigners applying for the renewal of their residence permits retain their rights for three months. Experimentation is necessary in the case of residence permits whose validity is less than four years. Depending on its results, the measure could become sustainable or be adapted.
ParlaMint-FR_2018-04-22-O1206.u1344	Mrs Elsa Faucillon has the floor to support Amendment No. 836.
ParlaMint-FR_2018-04-22-O1206.u1345	It was based on a recommendation of the Committee against Modern Slavery, which had found, in the context of cases of trafficking in human beings, that victims had often entered the national territory with the help of exploiters who had provided them with false identity documents. The absence of an identification procedure in France means that a victim of trafficking in human beings arrested in possession of false identity documents can be prosecuted and deported. As the UN Working Groups on Trafficking in Persons have pointed out, victims of trafficking may never be recognized as such. Even when identified, they can be treated as criminals and not as victims, both in the State of destination and in the State of transit or origin. Concerned about their fate, victims are also afraid of being exposed to reprisals by traffickers, which discourages them from demanding increased protection. The purpose of this amendment is to introduce into the Criminal Code a provision excluding the prosecution and punishment of persons who have been forced to commit offences in situations of trafficking in human beings.
ParlaMint-FR_2018-04-22-O1206.u1346	All right!
ParlaMint-FR_2018-04-22-O1206.u1347	What is the opinion of the committee?
ParlaMint-FR_2018-04-22-O1206.u1348	We share your concern about the protection of victims of trafficking in human beings who have committed offences in this context, dear colleague. However, your request is already met by the Criminal Code. Articles 122-2 and 122-7 of the Criminal Code provide, respectively, for the criminal irresponsibility of a person who has acted "under the influence of force or coercion to which he was unable to resist" and of a person "who (...) performs an act necessary for the protection of [his] person. In addition, victims of trafficking in human beings who file complaints are granted a residence permit as such. Thus, their protection is ensured by existing law. The committee's opinion is therefore unfavourable.
ParlaMint-FR_2018-04-22-O1206.u1349	Mrs Elsa Faucillon has the floor to support Amendment No. 810.
ParlaMint-FR_2018-04-22-O1206.u1350	It provides for the submission of an evaluation report of Order No. 2011-337 of 29 March 2011 amending the judicial organization in the department of Mayotte. Article 30 of the draft law, which is based on a logic of general suspicion, provides for significant restrictions on foreign nationals who are parents of French children, in particular with regard to the right to stay and the right to the filiation of their children. Unfortunately, these very worrying provisions are already in force in Mayotte. They seriously violate the right to private and family life, as well as the best interests of the child. These new provisions, which have not yet been assessed, could have the effect of infringing the child's right to identity, delaying the author of the child's recognition from exercising rights in the exercise of parental authority and undermining the application of inheritance rights. The report proposed by the amendment would list their impact on the parentage of children born to a foreign parent and on the applicable asylum procedure.

In December 2012, the European Court of Human Rights condemned France, stating that the specific nature of the ultramarine migration context cannot justify a derogation from respect for fundamental rights. This decision alerts us to a fact well known to those interested in overseas: CESEDA does not apply in the same way in Hexagon as overseas. Paragraphs 5 to 8 of Article 38 reinforce and accentuate these differences with regard to Guyana. Through them, the Government plans to increase from 90 days to 60 days the period between entry into the territory and the filing of an application for asylum in order to be entitled to a review of the application under a procedure known as normal by OFPRA and CNDA. The Government is actually proposing a budgetary plan to the detriment of the most fundamental rights of asylum-seekers, including the right to a serious examination of their claims. We propose to put an end to these unacceptable differences between the territories of the Republic. Moreover, we are aware of the particular situation in Guyana, in particular the migratory tension which may be felt there. [[Applause on the benches of the FI group]] But there are two paths that you never take into consideration, and which could greatly alleviate these tensions: first, a catch-up plan should be considered so that public services are at least equal to those of hexagon, and a co-development plan should be considered with the countries of the region. This would be an opportunity to set up ecological planning in a Guyana whose resources are known.

We continue to say that there is a need for a catch-up plan for public services. Social insecurity, which is very strong in Mayotte, fuels the civil insecurity that we see day after day. There are basic needs for investment, education and training that need to be addressed. Regional co-development with the Comoros is also essential. These amendments aim at ensuring the best possible equality of rights in the territories, starting with procedural rights and the right to a full and serious examination of asylum applications. On the contrary, the Government proposes that the application of the provisions of article 16 to allow the judge of liberty and detention to rule within 48 hours on the prefectural application for an extension of detention be excluded, so that the judge cannot rule beyond the seventh day of detention, a period prescribed by the Constitutional Council. We therefore propose to fix the administrative detention phase without the possibility of bringing the matter before the judge not at five days, but at 48 hours: it is common law. [[Applause on the benches of the FI group]] These amendments would also enable France to avoid a new condemnation by the European Court of Human Rights. We will continue to affirm this: Mayotte, like all ultramarine territories, must enjoy republican equality in terms of social needs but also guarantees of democratic rights and freedoms.

ParlaMint-FR_2018-04-22-O1206.u1372	The provisions of the CESEDA apply unevenly in metropolitan and overseas areas, especially in Mayotte, where derogations from the common law are the most shocking. Thus, access to the judge is limited in Guyana, Guadeloupe, Mayotte and Saint-Barthélemy. Overseas, the obligation to leave French territory is not automatically suspensive, as in metropolitan France. Some remedies are not even available. In addition, the conditions of detention are not the same: access to toilets and washbasins is not mandatory in certain detention centres, such as Mayotte. The real reason is cold behind the back; In 2016, the report of the Comptroller General of Places of Deprivation of Liberty called a cat and referred to the logic of "almost industrial" expulsion overseas. Human rights therefore weigh little and, in France, a foreigner does not have the same rights depending on where he is located. We propose that you remedy this situation.

ParlaMint-FR_2018-04-22-O1206.u1428	The draft law provides that, when the application for asylum is registered, the alien is now informed of the languages in which he or she may be heard during the personal interview with OFPRA. The choice of a language will thus be effective against him. This provision allows for greater efficiency in the processing of applications, but it does require a reorganization of services and the introduction of new techniques in order to limit errors due to the complexity of languages. This amendment therefore proposes to postpone the entry into force of this measure until 1 January 2020 in order to allow for this reorganisation of services or the introduction of these new arrangements, which may be provided for in the next Finance Act for 2019. A screening assessment will be carried out in July 2019, six months before the entry into force, to ensure that prefectures are able to correctly identify the applicant's language.

ParlaMint-FR_2018-04-22-O1206.u1590	[[Applause on the benches of the FI and GDR groups]] When you don't understand it, you can only stumble from one aberration to another. ParlaMint-FR_2018-04-22-O1206.u1591	That's right!

In the mines, in the North, our comrades knew this: those who were at the bottom, they were the last arrived; Those who were in the offices were the ones who had been here for the longest time. [[Ladies and gentlemen of the LR Group stand up and start to leave the Chamber]] The issue of immigration is necessarily a difficult one, in which the human situation of each individual must be taken into account. ParlaMint-FR_2018-04-22-O1206.u1600	We're sorry to leave you!
ParlaMint-FR_2018-04-22-O1206.u1601	What disrespect! The Minister of State is answering you!
ParlaMint-FR_2018-04-22-O1206.u1602	[[Applause on the benches of the LaREM and MODEM groups]] On the other hand, some believe that today borders should be abolished. Yes, Jean-Luc Mélenchon was right when he said earlier that the problem was first migration. It is therefore appropriate that we consider the overall development of the world as a whole and that the European Union in particular should assume its responsibilities in relation to the countries from which the migrants arriving at home come. It must give them the capacity to develop so that they can have a future and hope in their own country. That is what the President of the Republic and the Government are trying to do, which they are constantly arguing at European level, and I think we will move forward. [[Applause and "Congratulation! On the benches of the LaREM and MODEM groups. – Some Members rise and applaud standing]] But at the same time, how can we not see that we cannot have an open world? How can we resolve not to distinguish between, on the one hand, those who need protection, because they are refugees from the theatres of war, persecuted for ethnic and religious reasons, and, on the other hand, those who come here driven by misery? No, as you know, it would be impossible not to make this distinction, and we make it as human as possible. In six months I had the opportunity to say this: to those who will be refugees at home, we give the opportunity to build a future in France very quickly; At the same time, people who are rejected must not lose contact with their country. When we say, even if it has displeased some, that in the detention centres we will offer aid for voluntary return, we want those whom we have rejected not only to be expelled, but also to be able to rebuild a future at home. This is the position we stand for; I don't know if she's balanced, but I know she's fair and human. [[Applause on the benches of the LaREM and MODEM groups]] [[Ladies and gentlemen of the LaREM and MODEM groups stand up and applaud at length]] Ladies and gentlemen, we have to look at reality. For my part, it is always the reality that I am leaving: when Île-de-France, of which several Members are present here, concentrates 40% of asylum applications, do you think that we can allow those who will find themselves in this region to build a future? No, when I look at reality as it is, I do not see the possibility of beautiful encounters that Jean-Luc Mélenchon evoked. On the contrary, I see the territories being separated, I see people living side by side, and my great apprehension is to see them tomorrow face to face. That's what guides the line I'm at the Ministry of the Interior. One of your colleagues said earlier that other departments should have been convened. Yes, he was right: we must summon other ministries because it is through urban planning, culture, education that we will build this France of the meeting. I present here only one aspect of the Government's policy, but this policy must be comprehensive. It is both economic renewal, the renewal of our territories and the renewal of our cities that must be committed. That is how we will have the capacity to welcome and allow all those who live in France, whatever their origin, to think about the future for them and for their children in our country. That's the line we're following, that's the line you've adopted. Thank you!
